## Document 078: 2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/3 
2027 年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート 
～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 経団連による、2027 年春闘に向けての経労委報告原案が判明した。高い賃上げの「社会全体
への浸透」を掲げており、大企業を中心に高い賃上げが定着しつつあるなか、その動きを中
小企業などにも広げることに重点を置く姿勢がうかがえる。また、筒井経団連会長の発言か
らは、 ベア実施の検討をスタンダードとする従来の方針が維持されていることも確認できる。 
○ 流通やサービス、繊維などの労働組合で構成されるＵＡゼンセンも定昇込み６％、ベア４％
など、前年並みの高い賃上げ要求を掲げた。労使とも、賃上げに向けての姿勢にトーンダウ
ンはみられない。 
○ 一方、賃上げ率のさらなる加速を示唆する材料は現時点で多くない。連合の要求目安は前年
より強まったものの、経団連は賃上げ率の加速までは示していないとみられ、ＵＡゼンセン
の要求率は前年と同水準である。2027 年春闘では、 ５％近傍という高い賃上げ率を維持でき
るかが焦点となる。 
○ 日銀の植田総裁は、2027 年春闘で２％のインフレ率を前提とした賃金設定が行われることの
重要性を指摘した。2026 年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍化するなかでも高い賃上げが続くかど
うかは、２％の物価安定目標の定着や今後の金融政策を判断するうえで重要となる。

経労委報告原案は、高い賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」を掲げる 
10 月22 日のレポート（2027 年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも
昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所）では、連
合の基本構想や経団連の経労委報告骨子案などをもとに、2027 年春闘に向けた賃上げ姿勢は労使
双方とも前年からのトーンダウンはみられないと評価したが、その後、経団連の経労委報告原案
やＵＡゼンセンの要求方針素案が明らかになった。本稿では、これらの新たな材料を踏まえ、春
闘の初動のモメンタムをアップデートする。 
10 月30 日、経団連が2027 年春闘に向けて取りまとめる「経営労働政策特別委員会報告（経労
委報告） 」の原案内容が報道された。報道によれば、原案では賃上げの力強いモメンタムを継続

### Page 2

2/3 
し、その動きを社会全体に浸透させるために取り組みを強化する方針が示された。特に、中小企
業が継続的・計画的な賃上げを実施できるかが重要とされ、適正な価格転嫁に加え、大企業が持
つ人材や技術を活用して中小企業の収益力向上を支援する必要性が指摘された。また、ＡＩの普
及を踏まえ、働き手が生み出した成果をどのように評価し、賃金などの処遇へ反映するかについ
て、見直しを求めている。生産性向上によって生み出された付加価値を賃金にどう配分するか
は、今後の労使交渉においても重要な課題となるだろう。 
経団連はこれまで、2023 年を賃上げの「起点」 、2024 年を「加速」 、2025 年を「定着」 、2026 年
を「さらなる定着」の年と位置付けてきた。今回の原案では「社会全体への浸透」が掲げられて
おり、大企業を中心に高い賃上げが定着しつつあるなか、その動きを中小企業などにも広げるこ
とに重点を置く姿勢がうかがえる。2026 年春闘向けの経労委報告でも、中小企業における構造的
な賃上げや適正な価格転嫁の重要性が強調されていたが、今回の原案では、大企業による人材・
技術面での支援にも言及するなど、賃上げの裾野を広げるための取り組みをより具体的に示して
いる。 
賃上げに対する経営側の基本姿勢も維持されている。2026 年春闘向けの経労委報告では、ベア
実施の検討を賃金交渉のスタンダードと位置付けていたが、筒井経団連会長は今年９月７日の記
者会見でも、 「ベースアップ実施の検討を引き続きスタンダードと位置付けていきたい」と述べて
いる。ベアに対する従来の方針が維持されていることは重要だ。今春以降のイラン情勢悪化を受
け、原材料費や物流費の上昇によって企業の賃上げ余力が圧迫されることが懸念されていたが、
少なくとも現時点で経団連が賃上げに慎重な姿勢へ転じた様子はうかがえない。

UAゼンセンも前年並みの高い要求を維持 
労働側でも、主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針が明らかになり始めた。10 月 27 日、流通やサ
ービス、繊維などの労働組合で構成されるＵＡゼンセンは、2027 年春闘の要求方針素案を公表し
た。正社員組合員については、定昇込みで６％、ベア等の賃上げ分で４％を基本とし、賃金水準
が最低基準に満たない組合などでは格差是正分を加えて７％の賃上げを要求する。また、短時間
組合員については、制度昇給分に加えて５％、制度昇給分が明確でない場合には７％を目安とす
るとのことである。 
これらの要求水準は、いずれも2026 年春闘から変わっていない。６～７％という要求水準は依
然として高いが、要求率を前年からさらに引き上げたわけではないことには注意が必要だ。連合
は 2027 年春闘に向けた基本構想で「６％中心、５％以上」と前年より強い要求目安を示したが、
実際には前年の段階で、すでに６％程度の賃上げを要求していた産別組織もある。今回の連合の
方針には、こうした既存の要求水準を追認する側面もあり、ＵＡゼンセンの要求方針はその一例
といえる。

前年からのトーンダウンはみられていないが、加速しているわけでもない  
以上を踏まえると、2027 年春闘の初動のモメンタムは引き続き良好と評価できる。経団連はベ

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ア実施の検討をスタンダードとする従来の方針を維持し、高い賃上げを中小企業などにも広げる
ことで「社会全体への浸透」を目指す姿勢を示している。労働側でも、連合が前年より強い要求
目安を掲げており、ＵＡゼンセンも前年並みの高い要求水準を維持した。イラン情勢悪化に伴う
コスト増加が懸念されるなかでも、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢に前年からのトーンダウンはみられな
い。 
もっとも、賃上げ姿勢が慎重化していないことと、賃上げ率がさらに高まることは別問題であ
る。経団連が重視しているのは高い賃上げの継続とその裾野の拡大であり、賃上げ率の加速を示
唆しているわけではないだろう。また、ＵＡゼンセンの賃上げ要求は高いが、要求率自体は前年
から据え置かれている。連合の要求目安は強まったものの、現時点で賃上げ率の加速を見込むだ
けの材料は揃っていない。エコノミストの予想を集計するＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査（10 月調
査）でも、2027 年春闘の賃上げ率予想は4.96％とされており、前年実績の5.18％から小幅に鈍化
するとの見方となっている（数値は厚生労働省ベース）。 
当面の焦点は、５％近傍という高い賃上げ率を維持できるかどうかにある。もっとも、今後の
経済・物価情勢によっては企業の賃上げ余力や労働側の要求姿勢も変化し得るため、先行きを慎
重に見極める必要がある。当面、自動車総連や電機連合など主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針
が前年からどの程度変化するかが注目される。 
金融政策の観点からも2027 年春闘の動向は重要である。日銀の植田総裁は10 月 30 日の記者会
見で、基調的な物価上昇率が２％の物価安定の目標と概ね整合的な水準に達したとの認識を示し
た上で、2027 年春闘について、労使交渉でも２％のインフレ率が前提として織り込まれることが
必要との考えを示した。一方、2026 年の物価上昇率が過去２～３年より低くなる可能性を指摘
し、それが春闘の結果にどう影響するかを注視する姿勢も示している。 
ここ数年の春闘では、物価上昇に負けない賃上げを実現することが、高い賃上げ要求を支える
重要な要因となってきた。2026 年の物価上昇率は 2025 年と比べれば鈍化する見込みであり、こう
した観点からの賃上げ圧力はやや弱まる可能性がある。 
もっとも、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを前提として賃金を設定するよう
になれば、足元の物価上昇率が低下しても安定的な賃上げは持続しやすくなる。日銀は基調的な
物価上昇率が２％に「到達」したと判断したが、２％の物価安定目標が「定着」したとまでは判
断していない。その意味でも、2027 年春闘は、２％の物価上昇を前提とした賃金設定がどの程度
定着しているかを確認する機会となる。春闘に向けた労使双方の姿勢や実際の賃上げ動向は、今
後の日銀の金融政策判断においても重要な材料となるだろう。

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

highlights the company could help clients lower integration risk, as well as lower NRE

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

주가 추이

3Q26 매 출액 과 영 업 이익 은 비현 금 성 비용 항목 개 선 으로 시장 기대치
를 상 회 했 으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인 플 레 이션 등으로 연간 가이 던 스 는 
하 향 되 었 습니 다. 올해 생산 차질 에 따라 이 연된 신차 판 매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시 화 하 는 부분 에 주목 필 요 합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 Y oY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제 외 하 면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년 으 로 이연된 것 이 라 는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본 격 화 된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로 컬 업 체 들 의 공세를 방 어 하 는 모 습 을 
확인하는 것이 가 능 할 것으로 판 단 한 다. 노 후 차 종 중심 운 영 에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근 원 적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 30일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 233: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% Y oY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% Y oY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다. 
목표주가 (유지) 2,000,000원

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

## Document 245: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 - 기업.pdf

Date: 2026-11-04

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026.11.04   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 Company Brief

3Q26 실적, 원-달러 환율 하락에도 시장 기대치 대폭 상회 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 9.6조원(+59% YoY, + 28% QoQ), 영업이익 7,560억원 
(+26% YoY, +568% QoQ)으로 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 시장기대치(매출 
8.8조원, 영업이익 3,217억원)를 크게 상회했다. 당사는 Stellantis와의 캐나다 
합작관계 종료에 따른 보상금 등을 포함해 약 4,250억원의 일회성 이익이 반영된 
것으로 추정한다. 이를 제외한 영업이익은 약 3,310억원으로, 시장기대치에 대체로 
부합한 것으로 판단된다. AMPC는 미국 ESS 생산량 증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 
힘입어 전분기 대비 약 73% 증가한 4,169억원을 기록했다. 사업부문별로는 ESS의 
실적 개선이 두드러졌다. 상반기 배터리 패키징 병목으로 이연됐던 물량 출하가 
집중되면서 ESS 출하량은 전분기 대비 60% 가량 증가한 것으로 추정된다. 자동차 
전지도 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
33%로 증가했으며, 고정비 부담 완화에 따라 적자폭도 뚜렷하게 축소된 것으로 
판단된다. 소형전지는 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 출하 증가로 매출 
회복과 수익성을 뒷받침한 것으로 보인다. 
4Q26 ESS 중심의 수익성 개선과 수주 모멘텀 지속될 전망 
미국 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 확대와 AI 데이터센터의 전력 수요 증가에 힘입어 
빠르게 성장하고 있다. 특히 정책 환경도 비PFE 공급망을 확보한 업체에 유리하게 
작용하고 있다. 동사는 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스 콜에서 ESS 수주잔고가 약 
160GWh이며, 2028 년까지 캐파의 대부분에 대응하는 물량을 확보했다고 밝혔다. 
또한 현재 다수의 고객사들과 2028년 이후 공급 물량을 협의하고 있어 연말까지 
추가적인 수주 모멘텀이 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이를 바탕으로 4Q26에도 동사의 
본업 실적은 ESS 중심의 개선세를 이어갈 전망이다. 전분기에 반영된 대규모 일회성 
이익의 기저효과로 영업이익은 감소하겠지만, 이를 제외하면 출하량 증가와 가동률 
상승에 따른 점진적인 수익성 개선이 예상된다. 수익성 중심의 사업 효율화를 위해 
소형 IT용 파우치 사업을 축소하고 있다는 점도 중장기 손익 개선에 긍정적이다. 이를 
반영한 4Q26 매출과 영업이익은 각각 약 10.5조원(+63% YoY, +9% QoQ), 
4,150억원(흑자전환 YoY, -45% QoQ)으로 추정된다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 북미 ESS 수요 증가와 공급망 
재편에 따른 신규 수주 확대가 중장기 실적 성장을 뒷받침할 전망이다. 특히 ESS용 
생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화되면 매출 증가에 더해 고정비 부담 완화와 
AMPC 수혜 확대가 맞물리면서 수익성도 개선될 것으로 예상된다. ESS 의 역할이 
신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AI 데이터센터의 안정적인 전력 공급을 
지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 이에 따라 
동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 산업의 
성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다. 업종 내 실적 개선 가시성과 정책 
수혜가 뚜렷하고, 4Q26 신규 수주에 따른 주가 상승 가능성도 높다는 점에서 동사에 
대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다는 판단이다. 
Buy (Maintain)

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

종목추천 투자등급

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

artificial intelligence-related investment

continue to support a constructive

2 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015, 1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries, 2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks. 4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index. 5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.
3 Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, October 13, 2026.
4 Institute of International Finance, as of September 23, 2026.
5 FactSet, as of October 27, 2026.

MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.
Exhibit 4: Market Leadership Has Been Shifting Beneath the Surface.
A) October Saw a Sharp Broadening in Market Leadership. B) Value Has Quietly Led Growth in 2026.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

with demand, supply bottlenecks may
reinforce the investment case for the
companies providing the infrastructure
behind the AI buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

## Document 335: Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

7 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

## Document 345: France’s social demands run up against tightening fiscal constraints

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s social demands run up against tightening fiscal constraints.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Thursday’s nationwide mobilisation over wages and purchasing
power will add pressure on a French government already
struggling with weak growth, a fragile labour market and an
increasingly unmanageable budget. Its options for responding
to social discontent are narrowing as higher borrowing costs
further weaken the outlook for public debt
Charlotte de Montpellier
Student-led protests have grown into a broader social movement, putting
more pressure on the French government

Pressure mounts for action on purchasing
power
What began as a student-led protest has grown into a broader
social movement, adding to the government’s challenges as it
struggles to get its 2027 budget through parliament.
The mobilisation continued during the school holidays, with
between 320,000 and 500,000 people taking to the streets last
Saturday, fewer than in previous weeks but hardly the pause the

### Page 2

government had hoped for. The next test will come on Thursday, 5
November, when all the main trade unions will mobilise over wages
and purchasing power, with students and public sector workers
expected to join the protests.
Under pressure, the government is considering raising the
minimum wage by more than the automatic increase of around
1% due in January, possibly adding another 0.5 to 0.6 percentage
points. This would be the ﬁrst discretionary increase since 2012. But

employers are strongly opposed, as they are already facing higher
labour costs through planned cuts to social contribution
exemptions. A further rise in the minimum wage could therefore
encourage companies to limit recruitment or reduce their
workforce, at a time when the labour market is already fragile.
Private sector employment has declined for seven consecutive
quarters in annual terms and now stands 0.3% lower than a year
ago, representing a loss of almost 70,000 jobs. A discretionary

increase in the minimum wage would also have implications for the
public sector. It could raise the pay of around 15% of public sector
employees, adding further pressure on the public ﬁnances.
Part of the additional wage cost could also be passed on to
consumers, adding to inﬂationary pressures. Higher energy prices
have already pushed headline inﬂation from 3.1% in September to
an estimated 3.4% in October, while harmonised inﬂation reached
3.8%.

Budget talks are producing a larger deﬁcit,
not a solution
These pressures are making an already diﬃcult budget equation
even harder to solve. After 10 full days of debate and more than
2,300 amendments examined, France’s 2027 budget discussions
appear to be going nowhere, except towards a larger deﬁcit than
the government intended. While the government aims to reduce
the deﬁcit to 5% of GDP in 2027, after an expected 5.4% in 2026,
the measures approved so far have pushed it substantially further

oﬀ course. According to the Finance Ministry, the amendments
adopted during the ﬁrst reading have already worsened the

### Page 3

projected deﬁcit by €23.9bn (total deﬁcit in 2026 will be around
€165bn).
The vote on the revenue part has been postponed again, as MPs
continue to adopt measures pointing in all directions. Yet the
exercise increasingly makes little sense: the fragmented National
Assembly is unlikely to approve the budget, leaving the
government with two possible escape routes. It could use Article
49.3 to pass the bill without a vote, but would ﬁrst need to
negotiate with parts of the opposition to ensure that a subsequent

no-conﬁdence motion fails. For now, there are few signs of such
talks. If the motion passed, the government would fall and the
budget would be rejected. Alternatively, once the constitutional 70-
day deadline expires on 18 December, the government could
implement its original budget by ordinance. A two-stage solution,
with the ordinance followed by a corrective bill, is reportedly
gaining support among MPs, including some Socialists. The
corrective bill could accommodate selected social promises.

Weak growth makes the ﬁscal targets
unrealistic
The problem is that the government’s initial budget will no longer
be suﬃcient to bring the deﬁcit down to 5% of GDP. It was based on
GDP growth of 1% in 2027, an assumption that now looks clearly
too optimistic. The French economy contracted by 0.1% in the third
quarter, after stagnating in the second and shrinking by 0.2% in the
ﬁrst, leaving GDP marginally lower than a year earlier and France
hovering close to recession.

The details of the third-quarter ﬁgure were also weak: investment
fell by 0.6% and net trade subtracted 0.6 percentage points from
growth as exports dropped sharply, while inventories provided a
temporary oﬀset. The only real positive surprise came from
household consumption, which rose by 0.4%. But there is little
reason to expect this momentum to last. Consumer conﬁdence fell
by ﬁve points to 80 in October, its lowest level since June 2013 and
well below its long-term average of 100. Households’ assessment

of whether this is a good time to make major purchases dropped

### Page 4

sharply, while fears of unemployment increased. Most strikingly,
their assessment of living standards fell to its lowest level since the
survey began in October 1972. This points to much weaker
consumption in the coming months.
The outlook for investment is equally weak. Financing conditions
are deteriorating particularly sharply in France: according to the
latest ECB Bank Lending Survey, a net 27% of French banks
tightened credit standards for businesses in the third quarter,

compared with around 8% in Germany, no tightening in Spain and
an easing in Italy. Together with weak demand, higher interest
rates and uncertainty over taxes and public spending, this is likely
to encourage companies and households to postpone projects.
Combined with political uncertainty and social tensions, growth is
likely to be very weak in the fourth quarter as well. All in all, we now
expect GDP growth of just 0.3% this year and 0.5% in 2027, with

risks still tilted to the downside. Under these assumptions,
implementing the budget unchanged would leave the deﬁcit at
around 5.5% of GDP. Reaching the government’s 5% target would
require roughly €12bn in additional savings, on top of the €43bn
eﬀort already included in the budget, which looks highly unlikely in
the current political environment. And that is before including the
almost €500mn cost of the measures promised to high-school and
university students.

Debt is unlikely to stabilise anytime soon
All this leaves France’s debt dynamics looking increasingly
uncomfortable. The government had expected public debt to reach
121.7% of GDP in 2027, already a level at which a 5% deﬁcit would
be insuﬃcient to stabilise the debt ratio. With growth now likely to
be weaker and the deﬁcit higher than assumed in the budget, debt
will probably rise even further, and there is little prospect of
stabilisation anytime soon.

The sharp rise in borrowing costs only makes the arithmetic worse.
The French 10-year yield is currently around 4.9%, more than
155bp above its German equivalent, compared with a spread of
around 70bp at the end of 2025. Admittedly, the increase in market

yields feeds only gradually into the eﬀective cost of the debt as
existing bonds mature: the implicit interest rate on French public
debt is still around 2.2% in 2026. But this also means that a
substantial part of the rise in interest costs has yet to come
through. Fitch estimates that France would need to reduce its
primary deﬁcit from around 2.9% of GDP this year to just 0.4% to
stabilise the debt ratio. The adjustment required would become
considerably larger if the eﬀective interest rate on the debt

gradually converged towards current market yields.
And the 2027 presidential election is unlikely to provide an easy
reset. A new president could again ﬁnd her or himself without a
parliamentary majority, making the large and sustained ﬁscal
adjustment required increasingly diﬃcult to deliver. These concerns
prompted Fitch to take the unusual step on 30 October of bringing
forward its assessment and revising the outlook on France’s A+
rating from stable to negative, only a week after Moody’s

unexpectedly kept its Aa3 rating unchanged, albeit with a negative
outlook. Fitch explicitly pointed to weaker growth, higher borrowing
costs and a wider deﬁcit, together with political fragmentation and
social tensions that make sustained ﬁscal consolidation harder to
achieve. It expects French public debt to reach around 128% of GDP
by 2030, considerably above the roughly 123% projected by the
IMF.
All in all, tomorrow’s demonstrations will once again underline the

population’s frustration over purchasing power. But without
stronger economic growth to lift household incomes while
simultaneously easing the pressure on the public ﬁnances, the
government has very limited room to oﬀer measures large enough
to make a meaningful diﬀerence without making an already
worrying ﬁscal trajectory even worse.

## Document 347: GFHK - Apple F4Q26 review

Source PDF: all report pdf/GFHK - Apple F4Q26 review.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-05

### Page 1

Muted iPhone 18 Pro Cycle and Rising Memory Costs

[Table_Rank]

Maintained  Hold  
 Target price US$334

[Table_Summary] Lower TP to $334, Hold rating: Driven by investors being away from AI themes, Apple share 
price outperformed Nasdaq by 7.2% in the past one month. However, we continue to expect 
muted cycle of iPhone 18 P/PM with constrained volume of iPhone Duo. Additionally, memory 
cost hike will last longer than expected, driven by our view of tight DRAM/NAND supply, and 
we expect cost hikes throughout CY2027. For hardware, we still expect the iPhone 20 (2H27) 
to be a strong product cycle, thanks to new design of display, followed by Apple’s early move 
to TSMC ’s A14 in 2H28 (suggesting only 2 -years at 2nm). Additionally, Apple Intelligence 
remains the key to watch. Yet, the share price trades at 35x FY27E P/E, suggesting the stock 
isn’t cheap. By factoring in results, guidance and lower shipments/margins, we revise FY27E 
EPS by -10% and revise TP from $369 to $334, still based on 35x FY27E. 
Dec-Q margin guidance below expect ations: Apple delivered solid Sep t-Q results, with 
revenue of $114.5bn (+12% YoY) and diluted EPS of $2.1 (+14% YoY), both ahead of 
consensus. For Dec-Q, mgmt. guided to total revenue growth of 7 –10% YoY (vs. consensus 
of +6.5%), Services growth of 8 –9%, and gross margin of 45 –46%. The revenue outlook is 
constructive to Street expectations, though the margin guidance and Services deceleration 
were disappointing, reflecting cost hikes and mix headwinds.  
The iPhone product cycle mixed, with challenges in FY2027: Mgmt sees a strong start of 
iPhone Duo but remains supply constrained. Hence, we continue to expect the EMS build plan 
of 6.5m of iPhone Duo by end-2026 with limited upside, while the mainstream iPhone 18 P/PM 
to be 74.5m. As said in our earlier report of iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max Lukewarm on Sept 15, we 
believe the muted demand will weigh on CY1H27 EMS builds. Margin wise, despite p ricing 
actions taken earlier, margin will likely be under pressure, as the CEO Ternus characterized 
current memory pricing as “unprecedented,” with higher costs in December and beyond.  
Apple positioning the rebuilt Siri AI as a core differentiator: The CEO Ternus emphasized 
that the new Siri is deeply integrated and privacy -preserving and is fielding ~2.5bn requests 
per day. From a financial perspective, CFO Parekh noted that these Si ri AI costs are 
embedded in the December margin outlook, suggesting near-term gross-margin compression 
alongside the memory cost up-cycle.  
Risks: 1) weak demand, 2) competition, 3) product delays, 4) geo-tension.

### Page 2

Earnings Forecast  
Solid September Quarter Results  
Apple delivered a solid September-quarter, with revenue of $114.5bn (+12% YoY) and diluted EPS of 
$2.10 (+14% YoY), both ahead of consensus, reaching a record fiscal year of $479bn in revenue. Gross 
margin came in at 47.9%, aided by approximately one percentage point of tariff refund benefits (and a 
$0.05 EPS tailwind).  
iPhone revenue reached a September-quarter record of $57.6bn (+17.5% YoY) likely driven by higher 
ASPs, Mac rose a strong 24% to $10.8bn likely on edge-AI, Services set at $31.6bn (+9.8% YoY) which 
was below expectations, while iPad declined 10% to $6.2bn and Wearables fell 8% to $8.3bn. The 
company returned $28bn to shareholders. 
December Quarter Guidance Slightly Ahead in Revenue but Shy in Margin  
For the December quarter, mgmt. guided to total revenue growth of 7 –10% YoY ( vs. consensus of 
+6.5%), Services growth of 8–9%, and gross margin of 45–46%. Operating expenses are expected at 
$20.4–20.8bn, OI&E around $300 million, and the tax rate near 17%. The revenue outlook is 
constructive relative to prior Street expectations, though the margin range and Services deceleration 
reflect known cost and mix headwinds.

### Page 5

Valuation and Recommendation  
Hold with TP of $334 
We rate Apple (AAPL US) Hold and set our TP at $334 which is based on 35x our FY27E EPS estimate. 
We believe it is important to capture the 2H26 -3Q27’s iPhone 18 product cycle, given high market 
interest. We adopt P/E methodology as we believe the market now mainly focuses on the company’s 
earnings outlook, and we usually apply P/E method to downstream firms.  
We believe Apple’s ecosystem will remain strong and associated revenue durability is high. This is 
backed by Apple's installed base growth, double digit YoY growth in services. However, the market 
would wait for visibility for its Apple Intelligence. Meanwhile, the market will need more visibility on the 
higher-high retail prices for the iPhone 18 series due to the rising cost over tariff and 2nm/memories. 
That said, we expect its earnings growth to be  high-teens to 20% YoY in FY26E-FY28E, from single 
digit YoY in FY22-FY24. Considering >30x one-year P/E, the stock doesn’t look particularly cheap.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.
